## Step 2: done, 2026-09-09

Commit `1cd268f`, "RRC: one method, one source".

The four step names and a new one-line definition each are authored only in `read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json` and read through `recall/method.ts`. The door step cards, the briefing list, both "Before you start" asides and the walkthrough card now render from that one place.

- The walkthrough stopped coaching the two faults the card marks. It told a first-timer to read out loud, when step 1 is silent, and to say "what that piece is doing in the argument, evidence, conclusion", which is the pasteable label the judge refuses to count as a colour. It now states the four steps as a list, from the source.
- The asides were hand-written prose and had drifted into "say it back", step 2's retired student label. They are now `MethodSummary`, rendered from the source, above the four step cards.
- The fourth step is The Recall wherever it is named. The ready beat said "once when you have given your Read", which is the retired step name and not what that Space press does.
- "The move" was used on the Understood figure and every Your Read movement and defined nowhere. It is now said once, above the figures, where it also introduces the two numbers.
- Four em dashes out of `coach.ts`. "The retelling", internal vocabulary, off the card.

### Two corrections to this note's own audit

**The card's "Your Read" is not a violation, and Part 2 was wrong to call it one.** ADR 0025 (2026-09-03) is later than ADR 0012 (2026-09-01) and reinstated The Read as the second graded number: the reasoning half, against Coverage's memory half. They fail differently and the card is right to name both. Collapsing "Your Read" into "The Recall" would have erased the distinction the second number exists to make.

The cause was a stale record. `CONTEXT.md` listed "The Read" under The Recall's avoid-list as a retired name and never defined it anywhere, so every reader of the record, including this audit, concluded the card was using a dead term. CONTEXT.md now defines **The Read** and **the move** as their own entries, and a test pins The Read and The Recall apart.

**The authored rubric named the wrong half.** `coach.rubric[6]` read "Score The Recall against the captured key for that stimulus", which points the memory step at the reasoning key. ADR 0025 quotes that same sentence as "Score The Read". Restored. Nothing live was mis-graded: `grade-rrc-section` builds its own prompt and never reads `coach.rubric`, so the judge and `BAND_FLOOR` (ADR 0035) are untouched and no census re-run is needed.

### Verification

- 103 test files, 2739 tests, all passing. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
- New guards: `recall/method.test.ts` holds every surface to the authored steps and the CONTEXT.md avoid-list; `recall/SectionStage.coach.test.tsx` renders the walkthrough card, which the Activity Lab does not render and which is how it ran three stimuli deep into the wrong method unobserved.
- Browser: the door aside renders the four numbered steps from the source with zero retired vocabulary on the page; the Report Card defines the move at index 93 and first uses it at 364.
- `content:push`: 13 activities and 52 open assignments, 13 completed attempts left alone, `content:diff` confirms sync.

### Note on the repo

Another writer is committing to `projects/lsatu` on `main` during this work: `aa6bb7e`, `7471665`, `8ff610a`, `afb15a3` plus a merge and two Lovable "Changes" auto-commits landed alongside these two steps, and files outside the RRC area changed under the session. Nothing collided, and both RRC commits are intact on `origin/main`.

### Not yet deployed

Both steps are on `main`, which builds a Lovable preview only. The instructions text reached students the moment `content:push` ran, but the door, briefing, walkthrough and Report Card copy live in code and still show the old wording on lsatprep.study until a `deploy_project`.

## Step 3: done, 2026-09-09

Commit `3d3d999`, "RRC: an ending for the run the grader is slow to mark". The third P0.

=== TAIL ===
### A product call, not a CSS one

The ledger ticks stay under 44px **wide** deliberately. Twenty-two to twenty-six of them at 44px is 968 to 1144px inside an 820px card. They are 44 tall and clear WCAG 2.5.8's 24x24 with their gaps; meeting PRODUCT.md's stricter 44x44 would stop the ledger being a single-glance picture of the run, which is the whole point of the surface. Joshua's call if he wants it changed.

### Verification

104 test files, 2767 tests, all passing. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.

### The other writer pushed this one

`b1a3f37` reached `origin/main` without me pushing it: the concurrent session committed `776f69b` and `ece5480` (practice-test clock work) on top and pushed all three together. Same hazard as `afb15a3` earlier, in the other direction. Every commit in this run is authored "Joshua Fisseha", so authorship does not separate the two sessions. If both are going to run at once, they need separate branches.

---
name: push-only-your-own-commits
description: "Never push commits this session did not make; leave another session's or Lovable's unpushed work alone."
metadata: 
  node_type: memory
  type: feedback
  originSessionId: 810e4352-f17a-4f97-96c4-7e4935eb07bb
  modified: 2026-09-09T09:43:59.339Z
---

Push only the commits this chat made itself. If `git log origin/main..HEAD` contains
commits authored by another Claude session, another chat, or a Lovable sync, do not push
them. Stick to your own work, say what you are leaving behind, and stop, unless Joshua
asks you to push the rest.

**Why:** several sessions and Lovable write to `main` in the same repo at the same time.
Pushing a commit whose author has not chosen to publish it takes that decision away from
them, and on this project a push is the first half of a deploy, so it can carry
half-finished work toward production. Set on 2026-09-09 after this session ran
`.claude/safe-push.sh` and the push went out as `e1b2507..5fd1074`, carrying another
session's `70056d3` along with its own commit.

**How to apply:** before `bash .claude/safe-push.sh`, run `git log --oneline
origin/main..HEAD` and confirm every listed commit is one you wrote this session. If any
is not, do not push. This compounds with the existing rule never to `git add -A` in this
repo: a broad add sweeps up other people's in-flight edits, and a broad push then
publishes them. That already happened in reverse here, when a concurrent session's
`git add` swept this session's uncommitted motion work into its own commit.

Related: [[lovable-frontend-publish-is-separate]], [[lsatu-content-lives-in-postgres]].

| | Before | After |

The grass sways by `skewX` about the ground line instead of rotating each blade about its own base — verified `matrix(1, 0, ±0.028, 1, 0, 0)`, base drift 0px, tip vertical drift 0px, so the picture is unchanged. .ac-section-ready-retry,
.ac-section-ready-leave {
  border-radius: 9999px;
  padding: 0.4rem 1.1rem;
  font: inherit;
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: background-color 0.15s ease, color 0.15s ease, border-color 0.15s ease;
}

.ac-section-ready-retry {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-ink);
  background: var(--sec-ink);
  color: var(--sec-paper);
}

.ac-section-ready-retry:hover {
  background: var(--sec-chrome);
  border-color: var(--sec-chrome);
}

.ac-section-ready-leave {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-rule);
  background: none;
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
}

.ac-section-ready-leave:hover {
  background: #f1f5f9;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
}

@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .ac-section-level-fill {
    transition: none;
  }
}


/* ---- leaving the drill room -------------------------------------------- */

/* The one composed moment in the drill.
 *
 * The runner is a cold indigo mirror of the real section; the Report Card is
 * warm paper. The jump between them was abrupt because there was nothing
 * between them: the field cut to the card in one frame, at the end of
 * twenty-five unbroken minutes, which is the moment she is most likely to
 * remember and the moment that felt most like a page reload.
 *
 * So the field lifts rather than cuts. The paper card holds its position and
 * fades with it, the indigo warms to the ground the Report Card sits on, and
 * the handover happens underneath. Nothing waits on it: the save, the upload
 * and the grading request all run through these nine hundred milliseconds, and
 * the transition is over long before any of them are.
 */
.ac-section.is-finishing {
  /* The Activity Centre's own canvas, by value rather than by eye.
     `--background` is scoped to `.activity-centre`, which this element is not
     inside, and landing two shades off it turns a handover into a visible step
     at the exact moment it is meant to be invisible. Kept beside the token it
     copies so a change to one is a change to look for in the other. */
  background: hsl(38 36% 93%);
  transition: background 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
}

.ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-head {

234 recall/runStats.ts
import type { RecentSectionRun } from "../api/activitiesClient";

/**
 * What the launch surface says about a student's last few runs, derived and
 * nothing else.
 *
 * Every number here is read off runs she has already done. Nothing is scored,
 * nothing is predicted, and a number that cannot be established honestly is
 * absent rather than estimated -- which is why several of these are nullable.
 * The rule the whole file obeys: if we cannot know it, we do not print it.
 */

/**
 * One stimulus slot in a run, as the tick row draws it.
 *
 * Three states, not two, and the third is the important one. `notJudged` is a
 * stimulus she recorded and the judge returned no verdict for: our outage, not
 * her miss. Drawing it as an empty slot would tell her she skipped work she
 * actually did.
 *
 * `silent` is the fourth, and it is the one that is easy to get wrong. A
 * stimulus she never spoke into still produces a full grade row: the grader
 * writes `spoken: ""` with zero coverage and NO `ungraded` flag
 * (`grade-rrc-section/index.ts`). Treating the presence of a row as evidence of
 * recall would count her silence as work done and report "25 of 25" to somebody
 * who said nothing.
 *
 * The names deliberately avoid `carried`, which already means something else on
 * `SectionStimulusGrade` -- the NUMBER of details retained. Reusing it for "a
 * verdict exists" would make the two readings collide.
 */
export type TickState = "recorded" | "notJudged" | "silent" | "missing";

export interface RunTicks {
  /** One entry per authored slot, in authored order. */
  ticks: TickState[];
  /** Stimuli she actually spoke into, judged or not. Silence is not counted. */
  recorded: number;
  /** Authored slots in the section. */
  slots: number;
}

export interface SectionStats {
  /** Seconds on the clock for the most recent run. */
  lastRunSeconds: number | null;
  /**
   * Newest minus oldest, in seconds, across the runs shown. Negative is faster.
   * Null with fewer than two runs, where there is no trend to state.
   */
  trendSeconds: number | null;
  /** How many runs the trend spans. */
  trendRuns: number;
  /** Stimuli recorded across every run whose slot count is knowable. */
  recalled: number | null;
  /** Authored slots across those same runs. */
  recalledOf: number | null;
  /** The clock this section is run against, from the assignment. */
  targetMinutes: number | null;
  /** Runs finished inside the clock, of the ones measured against THIS target. */
  metTarget: number;

): SectionStats {
  const empty: SectionStats = {
    lastRunSeconds: null,
    trendSeconds: null,
    trendRuns: 0,
    recalled: null,
    recalledOf: null,
    targetMinutes,
    metTarget: 0,
    targetRuns: 0,
    totalRuns: 0,
  };
  if (runs.length === 0) return empty;

  const newest = seconds(runs[0]);
  const oldest = seconds(runs[runs.length - 1]);

  // Newest minus oldest. Getting faster has to come out negative, and the
  // subtraction the other way round reports an improving student as slowing
  // down.
  const trendSeconds =
    runs.length >= 2 && newest !== null && oldest !== null ? newest - oldest : null;

  // Only runs whose slot count is knowable contribute. A run with an unknowable
  // denominator is left out of both halves of the fraction rather than being
  // given a smaller one, which would inflate the percentage.
  let recalled = 0;
  let recalledOf = 0;
  let counted = 0;
  for (const run of runs) {
    const t = ticksFor(run);
    if (!t) continue;
    recalled += t.recorded;
    recalledOf += t.slots;
    counted += 1;
  }

  /*
   * The target cell is labelled with a CLOCK, so it has to count the clock.
   *
   * `within_goal` is stricter than it looks: it means inside the clock AND every
   * stimulus coloured and given a Read. Counting it under "25:00" would mark a
   * run that finished in twenty-two minutes as missing a twenty-five minute
   * target because of something that is not time at all. `within_time` is the
   * flag that means what the label says; older rows predate it, so the clock is
   * recomputed for those.
   *
   * Only runs measured against the SAME target are counted. A run from before an
   * accommodation changed was judged against a different number, and folding it
   * in reports her against a clock she was never given.
   */
  const comparable = runs.filter(
    (r) => targetMinutes !== null && r.section?.goal_minutes === targetMinutes,
  );
  const insideClock = (r: RecentSectionRun) =>
    typeof r.section?.within_time === "boolean"
      ? r.section.within_time
      : r.section.total_seconds <= r.section.goal_minutes * 60;

  return {
    lastRunSeconds: newest,
    trendSeconds,
    trendRuns: runs.length,
    recalled: counted > 0 ? recalled : null,
    recalledOf: counted > 0 ? recalledOf : null,
    targetMinutes,
    metTarget: comparable.filter(insideClock).length,
    targetRuns: comparable.length,
    totalRuns: runs.length,
  };
}

/** `1502` -> `25:02`. */
export function clock(totalSeconds: number): string {
  const sign = totalSeconds < 0 ? "-" : "";
  const s = Math.abs(Math.round(totalSeconds));
  return `${sign}${Math.floor(s / 60)}:${String(s % 60).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}

/** The trend, signed for a reader: `-6:52` is six minutes and change faster. */
export function signedClock(totalSeconds: number): string {
  if (totalSeconds === 0) return "0:00";
  const body = clock(Math.abs(totalSeconds));
  return `${totalSeconds < 0 ? "−" : "+"}${body}`;
}

  --rc-console-hairline: oklch(0.385 0.055 312);
  --rc-console-ink: oklch(0.968 0.014 312);
  --rc-console-ink-dim: oklch(0.815 0.035 312);
  --rc-console-ink-faint: oklch(0.7 0.05 312);

  /* ── The page: a violet-cast white, never cream, never grey ─────────── */
  --rc-page: oklch(0.979 0.008 312);
  --rc-card: oklch(0.995 0.004 312);
  --rc-fill: oklch(0.958 0.012 312);
  --rc-ink: oklch(0.235 0.032 312);
  --rc-ink-muted: oklch(0.475 0.03 312);
  --rc-ink-faint: oklch(0.505 0.035 312);
  --rc-hairline: oklch(0.9 0.014 312);
  --rc-hairline-soft: oklch(0.945 0.011 312);

  /* ── The four categories. Each is a KIND of feedback, not a severity. ─
     `-ink` is the text weight, measured to clear 4.5:1 on both the page and
     its own soft field; `-soft` is that field; `-lit` is the console version,
     which has to clear the same bar against a dark plum instead. */
  --rc-detail-ink: oklch(0.475 0.135 12);
  --rc-detail-soft: oklch(0.952 0.028 12);
  --rc-detail-lit: oklch(0.775 0.115 14);

  --rc-read-ink: oklch(0.46 0.095 218);
  --rc-read-soft: oklch(0.948 0.03 218);
  --rc-read-lit: oklch(0.79 0.1 210);

  --rc-colour-ink: oklch(0.475 0.105 72);
  --rc-colour-soft: oklch(0.955 0.04 82);
  --rc-colour-lit: oklch(0.83 0.13 82);

  --rc-method-ink: oklch(0.455 0.09 278);
  --rc-method-soft: oklch(0.951 0.026 278);
  --rc-method-lit: oklch(0.79 0.085 280);

  /* Sound. Not a category, and the only thing on the page that moves. Held at
     328 rather than nearer the reds: the playhead lands on the same line as the
     dropped-Detail chips, and twenty degrees of hue is not enough separation
     between "this is playing" and "this is what you lost". */
  --rc-signal: oklch(0.545 0.225 328);
  --rc-signal-bright: oklch(0.715 0.2 328);
  --rc-signal-soft: oklch(0.957 0.032 328);
  --rc-signal-ink: oklch(0.995 0.005 328);

  /* The two faint inks are set where they are because that is where they clear
     4.5:1 on their own ground, measured in the browser rather than assumed.
     Every label on this card is 10px, so there is no large-text exemption to
     lean on. */
  --rc-display: "Fraunces", "Iowan Old Style", "Times New Roman", serif;
  --rc-body: "Lexend", "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;
  --rc-mono: "IBM Plex Mono", ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace;

  /* Exponential ease. No bounce anywhere on this page: it is a page about
     being told what you got wrong, and a spring reads as a toy. */
  --rc-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);

  background: var(--rc-page);
  color: var(--rc-ink);
  font-family: var(--rc-body);
}

/* The card is read on a laptop in a long sitting, so the body column is capped
   at a measure rather than at the shell's container width. */
.rc__measure {
  margin-inline: auto;
  max-width: 72ch;

import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import {
  getSectionGrade,
  requestSectionGrading,
  startSectionFocusAttempt,
} from "../api/activitiesClient";
import type { SectionGrade } from "../api/types";
import { SectionReportCard } from "./SectionReportCard";

/**
 * The wait between finishing a section and seeing the Report Card.
 *
 * Grading is a post-section job: one transcription of ~25 minutes of audio plus
 * one judgement call per stimulus, run server-side after she stops. It does not
 * finish inside the request that ends the section, and it must not be tied to
 * this tab staying open.
 *
 * So this polls for the grade rather than waiting on a response. The ABSENCE of
 * a row is the pending signal (ADR 0019): there is no status column to trust,
 * and none she could set herself. If it never arrives, the honest thing to show
 * is that it is still being marked, not an error and certainly not a zero. The
 * sweeper picks up anything this tab missed.
 */

const POLL_MS = 4000;
/** After this, stop asking and tell her it is still being marked. */
const GIVE_UP_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000;
/**
 * How long to wait before asking for the grade a second time.
 *
 * The runner already asked once, fire and forget, and that ask can be lost: the
 * tab closed before it left, the network dropped, the function was cold. One
 * repeat after a decent wait recovers all three.
 *
 * The wait has to be longer than a grading actually takes. At 20 seconds the
 * repeat arrived while the first grading was still transcribing, nothing was on
 * record yet, and both ran to the end: two transcriptions, two sets of verdicts
 * on the same audio, and a card that showed the first while the database kept
 * the second. A real section grades in one to three minutes. The grader now
 * also refuses to start while another grading of the same Attempt is running,
 * so a repeat that does land early costs one request rather than a second
 * grading; this wait is what keeps that from being the ordinary path.
 */
const RETRY_ASK_MS = 120000;

interface SectionReportProps {
  assignmentId: string;
  onDone?: () => void;
}

export function SectionReport({ assignmentId, onDone }: SectionReportProps) {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const [grade, setGrade] = useState<SectionGrade | null>(null);
  const [stillWaiting, setStillWaiting] = useState(false);
  /**
   * Bumped by "Check again", which restarts the effect and so starts a fresh
   * give-up window. Before this, giving up was terminal: the screen stopped
   * asking for ever and the only way to find out was to leave and come back,
   * which is a thing she had to guess at.
   */
  const [round, setRound] = useState(0);
  const startedAt = useRef(Date.now());
  /** Minutes since she finished, for a wait that says how long it has been. */
  const [waitedMs, setWaitedMs] = useState(0);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (grade) return;
    const tick = setInterval(() => setWaitedMs(Date.now() - startedAt.current), 10000);
    return () => clearInterval(tick);
  }, [grade]);

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>;
    let askedAgain = false;
    /*
     * Measured per round, not from the first paint, so a "Check again" gets a
     * full window rather than giving up on its first tick.
     */
    const roundStartedAt = Date.now();

    const poll = async () => {
      try {
        const found = await getSectionGrade(assignmentId);
        if (cancelled) return;
        if (found) {
          setGrade(found);
          return;
        }
      } catch {
        // A failed read is not a failed run. Keep waiting; the row either
        // arrives or the give-up copy takes over.
      }
      if (cancelled) return;

      const waited = Date.now() - roundStartedAt;
      if (!askedAgain && waited > RETRY_ASK_MS) {
        // Ask once more. Losing the first ask is the likeliest way a finished
        // run ends up with no Report Card, and it is the one failure this
        // screen is in a position to repair by itself.
        askedAgain = true;
        void requestSectionGrading(assignmentId);
      }
      if (waited > GIVE_UP_MS) {
        setStillWaiting(true);
        return;
      }
      timer = setTimeout(poll, POLL_MS);
    };

    void poll();
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
      clearTimeout(timer);
    };
  }, [assignmentId, round]);

  if (grade)

      <div className="rounded-[28px] border border-border bg-[hsl(var(--ac-surface))] p-8 text-center shadow-[0_2px_24px_rgba(30,24,16,0.06)] md:p-12">
        <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-muted-foreground">
          Section finished
        </p>
        {/*
          * One live region over both states.
          *
          * The heading and the sentence both change without a page load, and
          * neither was announced: a screen reader user was left on "Marking
          * your run" with no way to know it had become "Still being marked",
          * or that the card had arrived. `aria-live` sits on the wrapper so the
          * pair is read as one update rather than two.
          */}
        <div role="status" aria-live="polite">
          <p className="ac-display mt-3 text-4xl text-foreground">
            {stillWaiting ? "Still being marked" : "Marking your run"}
          </p>
          <p className="mt-4 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
            {stillWaiting
              ? `This one has been marking for ${minutesWaited} minutes, which is longer than it should take. The marking carries on whether or not you stay on this page, and your Report Card will be on this section in the Activity Centre when it lands.`
              : "Your recording is being read back against every detail. That usually takes one to three minutes."}
          </p>
        </div>
        {/*
          * Past the give-up, two real actions.
          *
          * There used to be one button, "Done", and giving up was terminal:
          * the screen stopped asking, said her card would be there "when you
          * come back to it", and gave her nothing to come back with. "Check
          * again" restarts the polling and the ask; the other button says
          * where it goes.
          */}
        <div className="mt-8 flex flex-wrap items-center justify-center gap-3">
          {stillWaiting && (
            <Button
              onClick={() => {
                setStillWaiting(false);
                setRound((n) => n + 1);
              }}
            >
              Check again
            </Button>
          )}
          {onDone && (
            <Button variant="outline" onClick={onDone}>
              Back to the Activity Centre
            </Button>
          )}
        </div>
        {/*
          * What to do if it never lands, and the one thing she cannot supply.
          *
          * Said as a sentence rather than a "Tell Joshua" button on purpose. A
          * button has to be gated on `has_chat_access`, or it hands a student
          * without Messages a second dead end; reading that flag means mounting
          * `useUserPermissions`, which redirects on a lost session, into the
          * one screen whose whole job is to stay put while a grade lands. The
          * run id is the part she could not produce herself, and it costs
          * nothing to give her.
          */}
        {stillWaiting && (
          <p className="mt-4 text-xs text-muted-foreground">
            If it is still here tomorrow, tell Joshua and quote this run:{" "}
            <code>{assignmentId}</code>.
          </p>
        )}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

  background: hsl(38 36% 93%);

.ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-head {
  opacity: 0;
  transition: opacity 500ms ease;
}

.ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-paper {
  border-color: transparent;
  box-shadow: 0 1px 3px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.04);
  transition:
    box-shadow 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1),
    border-color 900ms ease;
}

/* What she reads while it happens. It arrives after the field has started to
   warm, so the sentence appears on paper rather than on the drill room. */
.ac-section-finished {
  margin: 0;
  animation: ac-section-finished-in 700ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1) 200ms both;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
  font-family: Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif;
  font-size: 22px;
  font-optical-sizing: none;
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 14;
  text-align: center;
}

.ac-section-finished span {
  display: block;
  margin-top: 0.75rem;
  /* #64748b is 4.76:1 on white and 4.17:1 on the warmed handover ground this
     sentence actually lands on, which is under AA. The chrome ink clears it on
     both. */
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
  font-family: var(--sec-sans);
  font-size: 0.875rem;
  line-height: 1.6;
}

@keyframes ac-section-finished-in {
  from {
    opacity: 0;
    transform: translateY(6px);
  }
  to {
    opacity: 1;
    transform: none;
  }
}

@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .ac-section.is-finishing,
  .ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-head,
  .ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-paper {
    transition: none;
  }

  .ac-section-finished {
    animation: none;
  }
}

/**
 * How much of it came back, in words.
 *
 * A Detail is OUR unit. It is how the judge is asked to mark, it is what the
 * coverage percentage is computed from, and a student has never seen one
 * enumerated: she read a passage and said it back. Printing "20 of 21 details"
 * hands her our machinery and asks her to care about the one number on the page
 * she cannot check (Joshua, 2026-09-08). The band says how it went; this says
 * how much of it she kept, the way a person would say it.
 */
function howMuch(percent: number): string {
  if (percent >= 100) return "All of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 90) return "Almost all of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 75) return "Most of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 55) return "About half of it came back.";
  if (percent >= 25) return "Some of it came back.";
  return "Not much of it came back.";
}

Three surfaces used to answer this question and they\n * did not have to agree:\n *\n * - The card read the stored `within_time` flag. Where a row predates the flag\n *   it is `undefined`, which is neither true nor false, so the card said\n *   nothing at all and a student got a time with no verdict beside it.\n * - The launch surface preferred the flag and recomputed only when it was\n *   missing, so the same run could be counted one way in the stat strip and\n *   read another way on the card.\n * - Nothing anywhere checked the flag against the figure printed above it, so a\n *   row whose flag and clock disagree renders \"28:32\" under a twenty-five\n *   minute goal and says \"Inside the time\".\n *\n * The grader writes `within_time` from exactly this comparison\n * (`grade-rrc-section/index.ts`), so for every run graded since the flag\n * existed, deriving and reading agree. {"action": "javascript_exec", "tabId": "seed", "text": "\n// Resolve the oklch ground to sRGB through a canvas, then measure the two\n// runner inks that land on it during the handover.\nconst c=document.createElement('canvas'); c.width=c.height=1;\nconst ctx=c.getContext('2d',{willReadFrequently:true});\nfunction srgb(css){ ctx.clearRect(0,0,1,1); ctx.fillStyle=css; ctx.fillRect(0,0,1,1); const d=ctx.getImageData(0,0,1,1).data; return [d[0],d[1],d[2]]; }\nconst lin=v=>{v/=255; return v<=0.03928? v/12.92 : Math.pow((v+0.055)/1.055,2.4);};\nconst L=([r,g,b])=>0.2126*lin(r)+0.7152*lin(g)+0.0722*lin(b);\nconst CR=(a,b)=>{const l1=L(a),l2=L(b);const[hi,lo]=l1>l2?[l1,l2]:[l2,l1];return +((hi+0.05)/(lo+0.05)).toFixed(2);};\n\nconst rcPage = srgb('oklch(0.979 0.008 312)');\nconst acCream = srgb('hsl(38 36% 93%)');\nconst rcCard = srgb('oklch(0.995 0.004 312)');\nconst secInk = srgb('#111827');\nconst secChrome = srgb('#334155');\nconst rcInk = srgb('oklch(0.235 0.032 312)');\nconst rcInkMuted = srgb('oklch(0.475 0.03 312)');\n\nreturn {\n  grounds: {rcPage:`rgb(${rcPage})`, acCreamToday:`rgb(${acCream})`, rcCard:`rgb(${rcCard})`},\n  onNewHandoverGround_rcPage: {\n    'sec-ink #111827': CR(secInk, rcPage),\n    'sec-chrome #334155': CR(secChrome, rcPage),\n  },\n  onTodaysGround_acCream: {\n    'sec-ink #111827': CR(secInk, acCream),\n    'sec-chrome #334155': CR(secChrome, acCream),\n  },\n  markingWaitInksOnRcPage: {\n    'rc-ink': CR(rcInk, rcPage),\n    'rc-ink-muted': CR(rcInkMuted, rcPage),\n  },\n};\n"}

{"command": "cd \"C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre\" && sed -n '476,515p' components/report-card.css", "description": "Read the ground block in report-card.css"}

## user

Tool result:
/* ── The ground the card lands on ─────────────────────────────────────────
   The runner's report phase is a `.activity-centre` page, which paints the
   Centre's warm cream (`38 36% 93%`). A violet card floating on warm cream is
   the exact clash the palette above was chosen to get away from, and the
   student never sees the card on the neutral ground it was designed against.
   So the report phase brings its own ground, tuned one step darker than the
   card's page so the card still reads as a card. */
/* Compounded rather than bare: `.activity-centre` paints its cream at the same
   specificity, so a lone `.rc-ground` wins or loses on stylesheet order, which
   is not a thing to leave to chance across a code-split build. */
.activity-centre.rc-ground,
.rc-ground {
  background: oklch(0.925 0.014 312);
}

/* ── The focus ring ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   `.activity-centre :focus-visible` forces an emerald ring on every focusable
   thing inside the module. Emerald is precisely what this card must not have,
   and it was reaching the ledger, the transport and every "Hear it" button.
   Same shape as the module's ring, in the colour that means sound here. */
.activity-centre .rc :focus-visible,
.rc :focus-visible {
  box-shadow:
    0 0 0 2px var(--rc-page),
    0 0 0 4px var(--rc-signal);
  outline: none;
}

/* On the console the ring has to clear a dark plum instead of paper. */
.activity-centre .rc__console :focus-visible,
.rc__console :focus-visible {
  box-shadow:
    0 0 0 2px var(--rc-console),
    0 0 0 4px var(--rc-signal-bright);
}

Tool result:
  });

  it("shows a stimulus she said nothing into as silent, not as recalled", () => {
    // The grader writes a full row with `spoken: ""` and no `ungraded` flag for
    // a stimulus she sat through. Counting the row would report her silence as
    // work done.
    const r = run({ stimuli: [stim(1), stim(2, { spoken: "" }), stim(3)] });
    const t = ticksFor(r);
    expect(t?.ticks).toEqual(["recorded", "silent", "recorded"]);
    expect(t?.recorded).toBe(2);
  });

  it("draws nothing for a run written before the words were stored", () => {
    const r = run({ stimuli: [stim(1), stim(2, { spoken: undefined })] });
    expect(ticksFor(r)).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe("the stat strip", () => {
  const faster = [
    run({ section: { ...run().section, total_seconds: 1450, within_time: true } }),
    run({ section: { ...run().section, total_seconds: 1665, within_time: true } }),
    run({
      section: { ...run().section, total_seconds: 1862, within_time: false, within_goal: false },
    }),
  ];

  it("reads the last run off the newest, through the nested summary", () => {
    expect(statsFor(faster, 25).lastRunSeconds).toBe(1450);
  });

  it("reports getting faster as a negative trend", () => {
    // 1450 - 1862. Subtracting the other way round reports an improving student
    // as slowing down.
    expect(statsFor(faster, 25).trendSeconds).toBe(-412);
  });

  it("reports getting slower as a positive trend", () => {
    const slower = [...faster].reverse();
    expect(statsFor(slower, 25).trendSeconds).toBe(412);
  });

  });

  it("leaves a run with an unknowable denominator out of both halves", () => {
    const known = run({ stimuli: [stim(1), stim(2), stim(3)] });
    const unknowable = run({
      section: { ...run().section, stimuli_in_section: undefined },
      stimuli: [stim(1, { item_order: undefined })],
    });
    const s = statsFor([known, unknowable], 25);
    expect(s.recalled).toBe(3);
    expect(s.recalledOf).toBe(3);
  });

  it("has no recalled figure at all when no run can be counted", () => {
    const s = statsFor([run({ stimuli: undefined })], 25);
    expect(s.recalled).toBeNull();
    expect(s.recalledOf).toBeNull();
  });

  it("counts the clock, not the method conditions bundled into within_goal", () => {
    // 1450 and 1665 are inside 25:00; 1862 is not. `within_goal` on the first
    // two is irrelevant here: the cell is labelled with a time.
    const s = statsFor(faster, 25);
    expect(s.metTarget).toBe(2);
    expect(s.targetRuns).toBe(3);
  });

  it("recomputes the clock for a run written before within_time existed", () => {
    const legacy = run({
      section: { ...run().section, total_seconds: 1400, within_time: undefined, within_goal: false },
      stimuli: [stim(1), stim(2), stim(3)],
    });
    expect(statsFor([legacy], 25).metTarget).toBe(1);
  });

  it("leaves out a run measured against a different clock", () => {

    });

import { act, fireEvent, render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { MemoryRouter } from "react-router-dom";
import type { SectionGrade } from "../api/types";

/*
 * The screen navigates now.
 *
 * A Report Card can start a fresh Attempt built from this run's weakest
 * stimuli, and that lands the student in the runner, so `SectionReport` calls
 * `useNavigate` and cannot be rendered outside a Router.
 */

[])[1]?.trim() ?? \"\";\n}\n\ndescribe(\"the handover out of the drill room\", () => {\n  it(\"warms to the same colour the report phase paints\", () => {\n    const warmsTo = backgroundOf(SECTION, \".ac-section.is-finishing\");\n    const landsOn = backgroundOf(REPORT_CARD, \".rc-ground\");\n    expect(warmsTo).not.toBe(\"\");\n    expect(warmsTo).toBe(landsOn);\n  });\n\n  it(\"does not warm to the Activity Centre's cream, which nothing downstream paints\", () => {\n    expect(backgroundOf(SECTION, \".ac-section.is-finishing\")).not.toMatch(/38\\s+36%\\s+93%/);\n  });\n});\nTEST\nnpx vitest run src/features/activity-centre/recall/section.css.test.ts src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReportCard.test.tsx --reporter=basic 2>&1 | grep -E \"×|→|Test Files|Tests \" | head -10", "description": "Guard the card verdict and the handover ground", "timeout": 400000}

The Report Card read the stored `within_time` flag and the launch\nsurface preferred it, so three surfaces answered the same question from three\nplaces and none of them checked the answer against the figure printed beside\nit. `recall/sectionClock.ts` derives it once, from the two numbers she can\nsee. The grader writes the flag from exactly that comparison, so nothing\nchanges for a run graded since the flag existed; what changes is everything\nelse. A row written before the flag was `undefined`, matched neither branch on\nthe card, and got a time with no verdict at all. A row whose flag disagrees\nwith its own clock now loses to the clock. In the harness, 28:32 against a\ntwenty-five minute goal said \"Inside the time\" and now says \"Over the goal\";\n24:00 said \"Over the goal\" and now says nothing, because she made it.\n\nA test was wrong in the same way. `runStats.test.ts` asserted that 1450 and\n1665 seconds are both inside 25:00. 1665 is 27:45. The fixture carried\n`within_time: true` to match the mistaken arithmetic and the code trusted the\nflag, so the error was invisible from both ends. The fixture is corrected to\n1465, which is inside the clock AND outside `within_goal`, so the assertion\nnow proves what its name claims.\n\nOne name for a number. \"Recalled 73/75\" on the door counted stimuli she spoke\ninto, not how much came back: a student could record every stimulus in a\nsection and recall almost nothing. It is Recorded, in the stat strip, in the\nrun table, and in `SectionStats`. The door's run table gains Held, the card's\nown section figure under the card's own name, so the number she remembers\nfrom one surface exists on the other. Per stimulus, the figure now sits beside\nthe words: \"95% held. Almost all of it came back.\" Joshua's call, 2026-09-09:\npercentages are allowed per stimulus and at section level. The older ruling\nstands and is untouched, because it bans Detail arithmetic, not the\npercentage.\n\nOne ground. The handover warmed to the Activity Centre's cream, rgb(244,239,231),\nand then the marking wait drew a cream Activity Centre panel on the violet\nground the report phase paints, and then the card arrived. Three grounds in\nten seconds, at the end of twenty-five unbroken minutes. The field now warms\nto what `.rc-ground` actually paints, and the wait is drawn in `.rc`, so it is\nthe card's own surface and the card replacing it is a change of content rather\nthan of place. Contrast improves either way: the handover inks measure 14.18:1\nand 8.28:1 on the new ground, the wait's 15.8:1, 6.34:1 and 5.63:1 on the\ncard's page.\n\nNote the audit said to warm to `--rc-page`. That is the card, not the page\nbehind it; `.rc-ground` is one step darker so the card still reads as a card,\nand warming to `--rc-page` would have left the same visible step one shade\nsmaller. The fixture is now 1465 — inside the clock *and* outside `within_goal`, which is the case that actually discriminates — so the assertion proves what its name claims.\n\n## One name for a number\n\n- **\"Recalled\" → \"Recorded\"** in the stat strip, the run table and `SectionStats`. It counts stimuli she spoke into, not how much came back; a student could record every stimulus in a section and recall almost nothing on any of them.\n- **The door's run table gains Held**, the card's own section figure under the card's own name, so the number she remembers from one surface exists on the other. The 2026-09-08 ruling stands untouched: it bans Detail arithmetic (\"20 of 21 details\"), not the percentage.\n\n## Three grounds in ten seconds, now one\n\nThe handover warmed to the Activity Centre's cream `rgb(244,239,231)`; the marking wait then drew a cream Centre panel on the violet ground the report phase paints; then the card arrived. The field now warms to what `.rc-ground` actually paints, and the wait is drawn in `.rc` — the card's own surface — so the card replacing it is a change of content, not of place.\n\nContrast improves either way, measured through a canvas: handover inks 14.18:1 and 8.28:1 on the new ground; the wait's 15.8:1, 6.34:1 and 5.63:1 on the card's page.\n\n**One correction to the plan.** It said to warm to `--rc-page`. That is the *card*, not the page behind it — `.rc-ground` is deliberately one step darker so the card still reads as a card. Warming to `--rc-page` would have left the same visible step, one shade smaller. A test now holds the copied value to the one it copies, since neither is in scope for the other's stylesheet.\n\n## Tests\n\n- New `recall/sectionClock.ts` with the derivation and the reasoning.\n- New `recall/section.css.test.ts`: the handover warms to the same colour the report phase paints, and not to the Centre's cream.\n- `SectionReport.test.tsx`: the wait is drawn on the card's ground, not the Centre's.\n- `SectionReportCard.test.tsx`: a run graded before the flag existed still gets a verdict; a stored flag does not outvote the time printed above it.\n- `runStats.test.ts`: corrected fixture, plus a contradiction case.\n- 106 test files, 2784 tests, all passing. `tsc --noEmit` clean.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nBODY\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -3", "description": "Push and open the Step 7 PR"}

The numeracy policy is already decided: percentages are allowed per stimulus and at section level, said identically on the door and the card, with \"Recalled\" renamed to \"Recorded\".\n@@NEW\n## Step 7: done, 2026-09-09\n\nCommit `2be5831`, \"RRC: one clock, one name for a number, one ground out of the drill room\". On branch `rrc-step-7`, [PR #102](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/102), not yet on `main`.\n\n### The Time verdict disagreed with the time above it\n\nThree surfaces answered the same question from three places and none checked the answer against the figure beside it. The card read the stored `within_time`; `runStats` preferred it and recomputed only when it was missing; nothing compared either to `total_seconds`. `recall/sectionClock.ts` derives it once now, from the two numbers she can see. The grader writes the flag from exactly that comparison, so nothing changes for a run graded since the flag existed.\n\n| Harness | Time | Goal | Before | After |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `state=faults` | 28:32 | 25 min | \"Inside the time\" | **\"Over the goal\"** |\n| `state=clean` | 24:00 | 25 min | \"Over the goal\" | **nothing, because she made it** |\n| a row predating the flag | any | any | *no verdict at all* | the verdict |\n\nThe third row is the one nobody had seen. `within_time` is optional; on a row written before it existed the value is `undefined`, which is neither `true` nor `false`, so both branches on the card were skipped and a student got a time, a goal, and silence between them.\n\n### A test was wrong in the same shape as the bug\n\n`runStats.test.ts` asserted, in its own comment, that 1450 and 1665 seconds are both inside 25:00. 25:00 is 1500 seconds; 1665 is 27:45. | Harness | Time vs goal | Before | After |

| `state=faults` | 28:32 / 25 min | "Inside the time" | **"Over the goal"** |

| row predating the flag | any | *no verdict at all* | the verdict |

That third row is the one nobody had seen: `within_time` is optional, so on an older row it's `undefined`, both branches were skipped, and a student got a time, a goal, and silence between them.

**A test was wrong in the same shape as the bug.** `runStats.test.ts` asserted in its own comment that 1450 and 1665 seconds are both inside 25:00. 25:00 is 1500 seconds; 1665 is 27:45. The fixture carried `within_time: true` to match the mistaken arithmetic and the code trusted the flag, so it passed for the wrong reason from both ends. Corrected to 1465 — inside the clock *and* outside `within_goal`, which is the case that actually discriminates.

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
